### v0.5.0-rc3
**The docs site now has to describe the software it ships with, and a script enforces the checkable half**

- **The problem was not a broken site, it was a true-looking one.** `mkdocs build --strict` was already clean and stayed clean throughout: strict mode validates that the site is well *formed* — every nav entry resolves, every internal link lands — and is silent on whether it is *correct*. So the site asserted a `scale_bar_helper.py` that has been `ueler/viewer/scale_bar.py` for months, a `_RENDER_TILE_LIMIT` that has never existed, and a Cell Annotation plugin "specified but not yet implemented" that ships and has its own tutorial page. Nothing checked correctness, and nothing built the docs strictly in CI either — `docs.yml` ran `mike deploy`, which does not build strictly, so even the formedness was accidental.
- **The claims were split by how they can be verified, so the expensive pass only handled what a program cannot decide.** Everything mechanical went into `tools/check_docs_consistency.py`: the distribution name in install commands, the stated Python range against `requires-python`, documented extras against `[project.optional-dependencies]`, every backticked repo path and GitHub blob link, every `make <target>`, every `UELER_*` / `ENABLE_MAP_MODE` variable against the code that reads it, code symbols named in the developer notes, UI labels against the real `displayed_name` / `description=` / `set_title` strings, Python examples parsing and importing only names `ueler/__init__.py` exports, nav coverage in both directions, and each developer note's `> Source:` link. Gated by `tests/test_docs_consistency.py` and `make check-docs`, and wired into `docs.yml` ahead of `mike deploy` — which also gains the strict build it never had. The remaining prose was reviewed page by page in churn order, with the review input bounded to the page, its `dev_note/topic_*.md` counterpart and `git log -p` for the matching code directory since the page's last commit.
- **The checker parses; it does not import.** `plugin_display_names()` and `ui_label_vocabulary()` read the plugin modules with `ast`, because importing a plugin drags in ipywidgets, matplotlib and anywidget — far too heavy for a docs gate, and a failure mode unrelated to the docs. `load_project_facts()` reads `pyproject.toml` with regexes for the reason `check_stable_rehearsal.py` already does: `requires-python` still admits 3.10, where `tomllib` does not exist, and three scalars plus a table's key list do not justify a dependency.
- **Two false-positive classes had to be modelled, and both were found by running the thing rather than reasoning about it.** The developer notes deliberately name code that is *gone* — "the `_AliasModuleFinder` / `_PrefixAliasFinder` layer in `ueler/_compat.py` … has been deleted" is the entire point of that paragraph — so `REMOVAL_NARRATION` skips symbol and path claims on a line carrying removal language, which keeps those paragraphs writable without an allowlist that would itself rot. And an early version flagged "**Cell gallery**" as a miscased "Gallery" when `description="Cell gallery"` is a real checkbox in `_chart_common.build_link_checkboxes()`; the fix generalised the rule from plugin labels to the whole UI vocabulary — every `description=`, `set_title` and `displayed_name` string — and flags a bolded term only when it is a **case variant** of a real one. That is the actual requirement stated precisely, rather than a heuristic approximating it.
- **Plugin placement was wrong on four pages (#121), and the current screenshot settles it.** `chart.py` and `heatmap.py` set `footer_only = True`, so they are skipped when the accordion is assembled and live *only* in the footer. `user-interface.md` listed both as right-panel entries, and it, `clustering-annotation.md` and `scatter-histogram.md` each documented a way of moving them — "enable **Horizontal layout**", "moves to the footer automatically when more than one scatter is active" — neither of which exists. `doc/GUI_preview.png` shows ten accordion entries with neither among them, and two footer tabs.
- **Mask controls are their own accordion section, not part of Channels.** `_rebuild_control_sections` appends a **Masks** section when `masks_available` and **Pixel annotations** when `annotations_available`; `user-interface.md`, `basic-usage.md` and `display-settings.md` all sent the reader into **Channels** to find mask checkboxes. The conditional construction is now stated too, because a missing section is an unreadable folder rather than a hidden setting.
- **Three more corrections a reader would have acted on and been wrong.** `ENABLE_MAP_MODE` is read at *module import time* — `_MAP_MODE_FLAG` is a module-level assignment captured into `self._map_mode_enabled` at construction — so setting it after `import ueler` does nothing even if it precedes `run_viewer`; `map-mode.md` and `faq.md` both said "before launching", which describes the failure rather than the fix. The Batch export **Cells:** picker shows at most 500 rows (`filtered.head(self._CELL_OPTION_LIMIT)`) with no indication of truncation, so the query filter is how you choose cells, not a pre-filter you then scroll. And `basic-usage.md` still described the pre-#125 tag input, asserting "not a scrolling list — there is no Shift- or Ctrl-click range selection" while `user-interface.md` on the same site described the filterable `ChannelPickerWidget` correctly.
- **"Follow main viewer" (#135) was undocumented, and it is the control most likely to be misread.** It is the *counterpart* of the **Main viewer** checkbox — one pushes the plot's selection outward, the other pulls the image's selection in — so confusing them is the obvious route to concluding that linking is broken. Now a direction table in `scatter-histogram.md` alongside the one-shot **Trace** variant, reflected in `cell-table.md`'s linked-selection diagram and in `clustering-annotation.md`.
- **Two developer notes were substantially wrong rather than merely stale.** `heatmap.md` called the Cell Annotation plugin unimplemented (it ships as `CellAnnotationPlugin`, with `CheckpointStore`, `parent_id`/`op` lineage and an anywidget tree browser) and called the heatmap a "cell-by-marker matrix" (it is cluster × marker of grouped medians, `df.groupby(cluster_column)[marker_columns].median()`, which is what makes it usable on a large table). `roi-gallery.md` listed 7 ROI columns including a `palette` that does not exist, where `ROI_COLUMNS` has 24 — the significant omission being `roi_kind` / `geometry`, i.e. shape ROIs, undocumented on both the developer page and `tutorials/roi-manager.md` despite a full Draw / Edit / Finish / Undo / Redo / Save shape UI that also reports a physical length.
- **Two new pages close the two real content gaps.** `develop-notes/plugin-development.md` and `develop-notes/mask-rendering.md`, from the two large `dev_note` topics that had no published counterpart — plugin authoring is the extension story and mask painting is a headline feature, and neither was on the site. Both carry material verified against code rather than transcribed: the batched `_max_dilate_labels_4` / `_dilate_within_labels` rewrite (#131) that replaces N per-cell dilations with two passes and is geometrically identical rather than approximate, and the `push_highlight` rule that stops a plugin echoing a received selection back over the user's own.
- **A `dev_note` was corrected, which is the tool earning its keep.** `topic_mask_rendering_highlighting_coloring.md` named a `GridChannelDisplay.update_mask_highlights`; no such method exists (`_update_grid_patches()` and `clear_patches()` do). The checker caught it while validating the new page drafted *from* that note — so it catches errors inherited from the source notes, not just editing slips. Corrected in place with a dated parenthetical rather than silently.
- **Mermaid diagrams now render.** Routing the fence to `pymdownx.superfences` with `format: !!python/name:pymdownx.superfences.fence_code_format` is the entire change: Material ships the integration, and adding a `<script>` tag or a third-party plugin would fight it. Caveat recorded in `mkdocs.yml` — Material's loader fetches mermaid 11 from `unpkg.com` at *runtime*, so a diagram is blank without access to that host, and diagrams therefore explain rather than being the only place a fact is stated.
- **The deferred screenshot from the entry below is done.** That entry recorded `docs/GUI_preview.png` as byte-identical to the *old* `doc/GUI_preview.png` and left it "for the next docs pass"; the two are now the same file, so the site no longer shows a pre-accordion UI while the README shows the current one.
- **One inconsistency found and deliberately not touched:** `pyproject.toml` advertises `Programming Language :: Python :: 3.12` while `tests.yml` still runs the 3.12 leg as `experimental: true` / `continue-on-error`, with the comment above it still reading as though the classifier were pending. UELer therefore claims 3.12 support that no blocking leg defends. Resolving it means dropping `experimental` *or* dropping the classifier — a decision about what to promise, not a docs fix — so it is recorded as an open item in `packaging.md` and left for the maintainer. Note this is the *other half* of the item the `rc1` entry below deferred: the classifier has since been added, the CI leg has not been promoted.
- **Files:** added `tools/check_docs_consistency.py`, `tests/test_docs_consistency.py`, `docs/develop-notes/plugin-development.md`, `docs/develop-notes/mask-rendering.md`, `dev_note/issue_tracking/docs_consistency_audit.md`; edited `mkdocs.yml` (mermaid fence, two nav entries), `Makefile` (`check-docs`), `.github/workflows/docs.yml` (strict build + checker before deploy), `dev_note/topic_mask_rendering_highlighting_coloring.md`, replaced `docs/GUI_preview.png`, and revised `docs/index.md`, `faq.md`, `develop-notes/{index,packaging,viewer-runtime,map-mode,export-pipeline,roi-gallery,heatmap,ome-tiff}.md` and `tutorials/{basic-usage,user-interface,display-settings,roi-manager,map-mode,export,cell-table,scatter-histogram,clustering-annotation}.md`.
- Verified: `make check-docs` → clean `mkdocs build --strict` then "Docs are consistent with the software"; `python -m unittest discover tests` → **1109 tests, OK** (1100 before, plus the 9 new). Mermaid confirmed in the built HTML — `<div class="mermaid">` on both new pages, zero mermaid references on a page without a diagram. `docs/GUI_preview.png` is a valid 1302×1133 RGBA PNG matching `doc/GUI_preview.png` byte for byte.

**The PyPI metadata now claims only what UELer does: a viewer, not a napari alternative and not an image-processing library**

- **The PyPI page no longer advertises UELer as a napari alternative, or as an image-processing library.** Two metadata entries left `pyproject.toml`. `napari-alternative` is gone from `keywords`: the keyword field is a search-term list, and nobody types that string into PyPI — it was a positioning claim in a field whose only job is matching the words a user actually reaches for, and it defined UELer by what it is not. The seven remaining keywords (`spatial proteomics`, `multiplexed imaging`, `image viewer`, `jupyter`, `MIBI`, `IMC`, `bioimage analysis`) are all things the tool is or reads. `Topic :: Scientific/Engineering :: Image Processing` is gone from `classifiers`, taking the block from ten entries to nine: that trove topic is the shelf a user browses for libraries that *transform* pixels — filtering, segmentation, registration, morphology — and UELer offers none of it. It loads, links and displays images and cell tables that ark-analysis, DeepCell and friends produced, so `Visualization` and `Bio-Informatics` say what it does without over-claiming, and a reader who filters PyPI by *Image Processing* is not looking for a viewer. Both were added in Gate B (2026-08-10), the classifier explicitly "beyond the plan — UELer is an image tool first", which is the reading now withdrawn.
- **The reason sits next to the code, not only here.** `pyproject.toml` already carried a comment above `classifiers` recording *why* there is no `License ::` entry, precisely so it would not get "fixed" back in; the absent `Image Processing` topic now gets the same treatment in the same comment block, since a missing classifier is invisible to anyone reading the file and looks like an oversight. The narrative version is a new key decision in `dev_note/topic_packaging_and_project.md` and its published twin `docs/develop-notes/packaging.md`, and the two historical Gate B records in `dev_note/issue_tracking/issue79_pypi_release_readiness.md` (sections **A1** and the classifier item) each gained an appended *Revised (2026-08-20)* bullet rather than being edited — the original decision and its reversal are both part of the trail.
- **The stale count in the Gate B status lines was retired, not falsified.** Both packaging notes said "ten PyPI classifiers"; they now read "the PyPI classifier block (ten entries then, nine since the 2026-08-20 metadata trim)", which keeps the 2026-08-10 fact true and stops the sentence from contradicting what a reader counts in `pyproject.toml`.
- Verified: `python -m build --wheel` then read the wheel's own `METADATA` — `Keywords:` lists the seven terms with no `napari-alternative`, and exactly nine `Classifier:` lines appear with no `Image Processing` among them. `twine check --strict` on that wheel **PASSED**, and the build artefacts were removed afterwards (`git status` clean apart from the four edited files). No code was touched, so the test suite is unaffected.

### v0.5.0-rc2
**A current GUI screenshot, and a README that points at the user guide instead of paraphrasing it**

- **The old screenshot showed a UELer that no longer exists.** `doc/GUI_preview.png` — the one image in the README, and the first thing a visitor sees under **User interface** — predated the layout the accordion refactor introduced. It showed the flat left column, where every selected channel contributed its own `Color / Min / Max` triple to one unbroken list with no grouping, no per-channel checkbox and no filter box; it showed **Chart** and **Heatmap** as right-panel accordion entries, from before the wide plugins moved to the footer; and it had no footer at all, so the very region the caption calls "bottom (optional)" was absent from the picture illustrating it. Replaced with a shot of the current interface: the left panel's **Channels / Masks / Advanced Settings** accordion with per-channel checkboxes and the channel filter, the footer carrying its **Heatmap** and **Scatter plot** tabs, the map-mode controls and their `Tiles / Canvas / Span` readout, the cell tooltip drawn over the viewer, **Lasso Select**, and today's plugin list (Cell tooltip label, Cell Annotation, Gallery, Cell Table Editor, Batch export, Go to, Histogram, Mask painter, ROI manager, FlowSOM). 1548×1101 → 1302×1133, and 896 KB → 754 KB despite the taller canvas.
- **Note that the plugin labelled *Chart* in the old shot is not the plugin removed in `rc1`.** It is `chart.py`, which is today's **Scatter plot** — renamed and moved to the footer, not deleted. The **Chart (heatmap)** duplicate that `v0.5.0-rc1` removed never appears in either screenshot, so nothing about this image swap documents that removal; the two changes happen to touch adjacent parts of the same UI and are otherwise unrelated.
- **The README's `User interface` section stopped duplicating the user guide.** Two prose blocks — **Overall Settings** (Cache Size, Select Image, channel selection, *No image (masks only)*, marker sets, the control accordion, annotations, masks) and **Tools & Plugins** (Mask Painter, ROI Manager, wide plugins) — described panels that `docs/tutorials/user-interface.md` documents in more depth, with per-plugin pages behind it. Two copies of the same reference text is one copy that silently goes stale, and this one had: the README still called the footer example "horizontal heatmap or gallery extensions" and named plugins in title case they no longer use. Both blocks are replaced by a single link to the versioned guide, and the four-region layout summary above it stays, since that is the caption for the screenshot rather than reference material. Each dropped item was checked to exist in the guide first — Cache Size (`:28`), *No image (masks only)* (`:81`), marker sets (`:49`), the right-panel accordion and Mask painter / ROI manager / Batch export entries (`:85`–`:102`), and **Horizontal layout** moving the Heatmap to the footer (`:111`).
- **The `ueler-viewer` / `ueler` name split is now stated, not justified — in both places it appears.** The README's install section carried a paragraph explaining *why* the distribution is `ueler-viewer` (PyPI administratively prohibits the name `ueler`; the `scikit-image`/`skimage` and `opencv-python`/`cv2` analogy), and `docs/installation.md` carried the same reasoning again as the body of its `!!! info` admonition. The fact a reader needs is the mapping between the two names, which the heading already gives them; the history of how PyPI's name policy produced it changes nothing they type. So the README keeps only its bold one-liner, and the admonition keeps only its title — an `!!! info` with no indented body renders as a title-only info box, which is exactly the callout wanted here. Verified in the built HTML: `<div class="admonition info">` now contains just the `admonition-title` paragraph.
- **The trailing `Earlier Updates` section is gone.** The **New Update** block already closes with a link to this file, so the README carried two consecutive pointers at the same document.
- **One inconsistency found and deliberately not touched:** `docs/GUI_preview.png`, which the user guide embeds at `docs/tutorials/user-interface.md:6`, is byte-identical to the *old* `doc/GUI_preview.png` and was not updated — the README now shows the current interface while the documentation site still shows the pre-accordion one. It is a second copy of the same asset under a different path rather than a shared file, so refreshing it is a separate edit; left for the next docs pass.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **1100 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped" (unchanged — no code was touched), plus a clean `mkdocs build --strict`. The new `doc/GUI_preview.png` is a valid 1302×1133 RGBA PNG, and the user-guide link resolves to a page that exists under the `mike` `latest` alias (`docs/tutorials/user-interface.md`).

### v0.5.0-rc1
**The install docs now describe two channels: stable from PyPI, pre-releases from TestPyPI**

- **Documentation only — this is the flip that `dev_note/release_procedure.md` step B4 asks for, done ahead of the tag rather than after it.** No code, no `pyproject.toml`, nothing that ships in the wheel: `docs/installation.md` and `README.md` only, both of which the stable-rehearsal gate leaves free to change. The pages had documented a single install path — TestPyPI, with `--pre` and a second index — because at the time PyPI had no `ueler-viewer` project at all. They now document the steady state the release pipeline actually implements: `pip install ueler-viewer` from **PyPI** for a stable release, and the long TestPyPI command only for a preview.
- **The two channels are not two mirrors, and the docs now say which is which.** `tools/release_channel.py` sends every tag to TestPyPI and only a stable one onward to PyPI, so TestPyPI carries the complete release history while PyPI carries the stable subset — meaning a pre-release is available *only* from TestPyPI, and that is the only reason a user should ever type the longer command. `docs/installation.md` states this as a two-row table under **Option A — Install with pip**, with `### Stable releases (PyPI)` and `### Pre-releases (TestPyPI)` beneath it.
- **`--pre` moved from "required for now" to "what selects a pre-release".** The old text explained the flag as a temporary consequence of UELer being pre-release (*"`--pre` is required while the version is a pre-release"*), which stops making sense the moment a stable version exists on PyPI. It is now described by what it does: without it pip's resolver ignores every `alpha`, `beta` and `rc`, which is exactly why the stable command does not carry it and the TestPyPI one must.
- **Two things a reader coming off a preview needs, which the page did not have.** Pinning one candidate (`ueler-viewer==0.6.0rc1`), with the note that PEP 440 normalises the tag spelling — a user reading `v0.6.0-rc1` in a release note cannot type it back verbatim. And returning to the stable channel: `pip install --upgrade ueler-viewer` will **not** move you from a newer pre-release to an older stable release, so the page says to `pip uninstall ueler-viewer` first. Both are consequences of the routing rule that only surface once both channels are live.
- **The `scikit-image>=0.19` troubleshooting admonition is now scoped to the command it belongs to.** TestPyPI hosting an empty `scikit-image` project is only a problem for a resolve that consults TestPyPI, so the warning (and the `uv --index-strategy unsafe-best-match` workaround) now opens with "Specific to the TestPyPI command" instead of reading as a general install hazard. Left in place otherwise, including the `pip uninstall ueler` warning for anyone still holding the old `ueler` distribution.
- **Navigation and stale anchors.** Added a short **Which install do I want?** list at the top (use it / preview it / modify it), renamed *Option A — Install from TestPyPI* to *Option A — Install with pip* since it is no longer one index, and fixed the "Updating UELer" cross-link, which still pointed at `#option-a-install-from-testpypi`. Verified by building the site: `mkdocs build --strict` is clean and every in-page `href="#…"` on the installation page resolves to a rendered heading id.
- **`README.md` follows the same split** — a plain `pip install ueler-viewer` block with the extras, a `#### Pre-releases (TestPyPI)` subsection pointing at the installation page for the details, and an **Upgrade UELer** section that now gives the stable and the pre-release upgrade command separately instead of only the TestPyPI one.
- **One inconsistency found and deliberately not touched:** `pyproject.toml` has `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.13"` and CI tests 3.10/3.11/3.12, but its `Programming Language :: Python` classifiers stop at 3.11 — despite the comment above them saying they must stay in step with `requires-python`. Fixing it edits `pyproject.toml`, which would invalidate the `v0.5.0-rc1` rehearsal and force an `rc2`, so it is left for the next code-bearing change. The prose in both documents says 3.10–3.12, matching `requires-python` and the test matrix.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **1100 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped" (unchanged — no code was touched), plus a clean `mkdocs build --strict`.

**Removed the "Chart (heatmap)" plugin**

- **What it was, and why it was not the Heatmap.** `ueler/viewer/plugin/chart_heatmap.py` was a near-duplicate of the Scatter plot plugin (`chart.py`) that presented itself in the footer as **"Chart (heatmap)"** — a second scatter/histogram panel whose only distinguishing feature was that its axes came from the Heatmap plugin's `heatmap_data` (clusters × markers) instead of the cell table. It shipped as a third footer tab next to Scatter plot and Heatmap and did nothing either of them could not, which is why `dev_note/issue_tracking/issue112_separate_histogram_plugin.md` already listed it under "out of scope / notes" as a stale duplicate "worth removing in a separate cleanup". This is that cleanup. The actual **Heatmap** plugin (`heatmap.py`, `heatmap_layers.py`, `heatmap_adapter.py`) is untouched, as are Scatter plot and Histogram.
- **Nothing depended on it, and that was verified rather than assumed.** The plugin was a pure consumer in both directions: it *read* `SidePlots.heatmap_output.heatmap_data` and *pushed* selections into `cell_gallery_output`, while no module anywhere read `chart_heatmap_output`. It also never imported `_chart_common.py`, so the shared scatter/histogram helpers lose no caller and the `_bounded_panel_layout` / `_content_control_layout` helpers it defined were private to the file. The only import of the module outside itself was its own test.
- **Deleting the file is the whole removal, because plugin discovery is a directory scan.** `main_viewer.dynamically_load_plugins` (`main_viewer.py:1940`) walks `os.listdir` over `ueler/viewer/plugin/` and instantiates every `PluginBase` subclass it finds in each non-underscore module — there is no registry, ordering list, or `allow_plugins` entry to edit, and no import site to unwire. The flip side of that implicitness is that a missed reference would not fail at import time but at runtime, so the residual grep over `ueler/`, `tests/` and `tools/` was run as a gate rather than a formality (clean), and the loader's module list was printed to confirm `chart_heatmap` is gone from it while the other nineteen remain.
- **Files:** deleted `ueler/viewer/plugin/chart_heatmap.py` (577 lines) and `tests/test_chart_heatmap_footer.py`; updated the footer-only section of `dev_note/topic_plugin_development.md`, which named the plugin as one of three current users of `footer_only = True`. Historical records that mention it — the #121, #112, #119, #125 and #135 tracking notes, `dev_note/github_issues.md`, `Packaging_plan.md`, `Todos.md` and the earlier entries below — are left as written, since they describe what was true at the time.
- **One harmless leftover on disk:** datasets opened with an earlier version keep an orphaned `.UELer/Chart (heatmap)_widget_states.json`. Nothing reads it any more; no cleanup code was added.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **1100 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped", against a pre-change baseline of **1102 tests, OK** — a difference of exactly the two deleted tests, with no other test changing state.

### v0.5.0-alpha2
**A tag now routes itself: pre-releases to TestPyPI, stable releases to PyPI (#79)**

*Lands after the `v0.5.0-alpha2` tag was cut; it changes the release pipeline only, and no code under `ueler/`.*

- **The routing rule is one predicate, and it needed no special-casing.** `tools/release_channel.py` decides the target index from `packaging.version.Version(tag).is_prerelease` alone: a pre-release goes to TestPyPI, anything else goes to TestPyPI and then PyPI. The existing tags serve as free test data for that predicate — not as releases to revisit — and it classifies all nine correctly, because the SemVer spellings in use (`-alpha`, `-alphaN`, `-rcN`) normalise to PEP 440 pre-release segments while a plain `vX.Y.Z` does not — the same normalisation `check_release_tag.py` already relies on. It also handles spellings not yet used: `-beta1` and `-dev1` are pre-releases, and `v1.0.0.post1` is *not*, so a post-release of a stable version correctly reaches the real index instead of being quietly diverted. The predicate lives in a script rather than a shell expression inside the YAML because a wrong answer burns a version number permanently, and this way it is unit-tested.
- **This removes `release.yml`'s deliberate asymmetry, so it had to be replaced by something stronger, not just deleted.** The old header comment promised "There is no path where a `git push --tags` reaches the real index by itself", and that promise was load-bearing: PyPI is append-only, so a mistyped `v0.5.0` used to cost nothing and now would burn `0.5.0` for good. What replaced it is a claim about the artifact rather than about the number of forms a human fills in — plus a required reviewer on the `pypi` environment, which turns the release from "run a second workflow by hand" into "approve a run that is already built and verified". A manual dispatch only ever proved that someone clicked.
- **A stable release must be the promotion of a release candidate TestPyPI already serves.** `tools/check_stable_rehearsal.py` refuses `vX.Y.Z` unless an `rc` tag exists for the same version, the **highest** such rc is served by TestPyPI, and everything that ships in the wheel is unchanged between the two tags. Each of those three clauses answers a specific way the check could have been fooled: accepting merely *some* rc would let `rc1` vouch for a release that `rc2` changed; trusting the tag alone would accept a candidate whose upload step failed, so the script asks `https://test.pypi.org/pypi/ueler-viewer/json` and fails closed on a network error; and an alpha does not count as a rehearsal at all, because a preview says "look at this" while a candidate says "this is what I intend to ship".
- **"Unchanged" cannot mean the same commit, and that constraint shaped the whole check.** `check_release_tag.py` requires the tag, `pyproject.toml [project] version`, `ueler.__version__` and both artifact filenames to describe one release — so the rc declares `0.5.0rc1` where the stable declares `0.5.0`, one commit cannot declare both, and the artifacts cannot be byte-identical either (filename, `METADATA`'s `Version:` and `__version__` all move). Any implementation comparing whole files or whole trees would fail on every correct release. The comparison is therefore scoped to what lands in the wheel — `ueler/**` and `pyproject.toml` — permitting only the `__version__` and `version` lines to differ, and additionally checking that the removed value normalises to the rc version and the added value to the stable one. Without that last clause, "only the version line changed" would be satisfied by a version line changed to anything at all.
- **Freezing `pyproject.toml` matters as much as freezing the code.** A dependency floor edited between the candidate and the release changes the wheel's metadata and invalidates the rehearsal even though no Python source moved — `tests/test_stable_rehearsal.py` covers exactly that case. Everything outside those two paths stays free: `doc/log.md`, `README.md`, `docs/**`, `dev_note/**` and `tests/**` are all expected to change on the way to a stable release and none of them affect what a user installs. That allowed set is precisely what the version-bump skill touches on an rc → stable bump, so the guard fits the existing tooling rather than fighting it.
- **The ancestry check was designed, then dropped.** Requiring the rc to be an ancestor of the stable tag looked like the natural extra safeguard, and under the new practice it will nearly always hold, since the release commit sits directly on top of the candidate. It is still not enforced: the content comparison is strictly stronger for the actual question — do the shipped bytes match — while `git merge-base` can fail for reasons that say nothing about the artifact, such as a rebase or a shallow fetch. As a gate it would add a failure mode without adding evidence, so it prints as a note.
- **Every publishing run passes through TestPyPI, stable releases included.** `pypi` declares `needs: testpypi`, so the cheapest possible pre-flight always runs before the irreversible upload and the failure direction is the safe one. This also fixed a trap in the first draft: making the TestPyPI leg conditional on the routing decision would have *skipped* it on a manual dispatch to PyPI, and a job whose `needs` was skipped is itself skipped — which would have silently disabled the manual path. Keeping TestPyPI on every path also keeps it a complete mirror of release history, which is what makes the next release's rehearsal check meaningful.
- **This is a change in release practice, not only in CI, and it applies going forward only.** Nothing already released is revisited or re-validated. From the next stable tag on: after the final rc no shipped file may change, so any fix, however small, needs a new rc tag and a new TestPyPI publish, and the stable release commit is a version bump plus documentation and nothing else. Since the `0.5.0` line has only alphas, shipping `0.5.0` will require pushing `v0.5.0-rc1` and letting it publish first — a good property for an upload that also creates the PyPI project.
- **The `route` job has no `needs`,** so an unreleasable tag fails in seconds rather than after the full test matrix. It rejects a tag that is not valid PEP 440 and a tag carrying a local version segment (`v0.5.0+dirty`) — PyPI refuses local versions outright, so catching it early costs nothing. `verify` gained `fetch-depth: 0`, without which the default shallow single-ref checkout cannot see the rc tag or diff two trees, and `make check-rehearsal TAG=v0.6.0` runs the same gate locally before tagging.
- **Confirmed against the live index while building this:** `https://test.pypi.org/pypi/ueler-viewer/json` reports `0.5.0a2`, so the `v0.5.0-alpha2` tag push did publish and did claim the name on TestPyPI; `https://pypi.org/simple/ueler-viewer/` still returns 404, so the PyPI project does not exist and its Trusted Publisher must be configured as a *pending* one before the first stable tag.
- **Planning:** `dev_note/issue_tracking/issue79_release_channel_routing.md`, including the rejected alternatives and the two settings that must be configured in the GitHub UI (the `pypi` required reviewer, the pending PyPI publisher) before this can be used.
- **29 new tests** across `tests/test_release_channel.py` (the historical-tag routing table, spelling normalisation, and every rejection) and `tests/test_stable_rehearsal.py` (a throwaway git repository rather than a mocked `git`, because the subtle part is what a real rc → stable diff looks like). Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **1102 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped".

**The distribution is now `ueler-viewer`; the import name is still `ueler` (#79)**

- **PyPI will not accept the name `ueler`, and the reason is almost certainly `euler`.** The pending-publisher form answers "This project name isn't allowed" — warehouse's response for an administratively **prohibited** name, not a taken one (`ueler` 404s on both `/pypi/ueler/json` and `/simple/ueler/`). Measured while settling this: **`euler` is a live PyPI project** (1.0.3, 3 releases, Euler-angle conversion) and does not exist on TestPyPI, which is exactly why TestPyPI accepted `ueler` and PyPI does not. That reconciles the two candidate explanations in the #79 audit rather than choosing between them: the *automated* similarity check cannot be what fires, because ultranormalisation folds confusable *characters* and never a transposition of letter order, so the block is a manual admin entry whose likely motive is protecting `euler` from typo-squatting. A prohibition that exists to protect a live project is the kind that does not get lifted on request, so `ueler-viewer` is planned as the long-lived name rather than a placeholder.
- **The rename touched one line of `pyproject.toml` and no code at all.** The distribution name (`[project] name`, what `pip install` asks for) and the import name (the `ueler/` package directory) are independent, so every `import ueler` in the package, the tests and `script/run_ueler.ipynb` is untouched. `ueler.__version__` was checked specifically: it is a literal in `ueler/__init__.py`, not an `importlib.metadata` lookup, so there was no distribution-name dependency to break — and nothing in `ueler/` uses `importlib.metadata` or `pkg_resources` at all. Verified against the built wheel: `Name: ueler-viewer`, `top_level.txt` → `ueler`. Precedent: `scikit-image`/`skimage`, `opencv-python`/`cv2`, `pillow`/`PIL`.
- **One name on both indexes, rather than `ueler` on TestPyPI and `ueler-viewer` on PyPI.** The dual arrangement was considered and rejected on cost: the name is baked into the artifact filename, `METADATA`'s `Name:` and `RECORD`, so one build carries exactly one name — meaning two separate builds, a `pyproject.toml`-rewriting build tool, two artifacts through `release.yml`'s job graph, and a `--expect-dist-name` guard to stop the wrong-named wheel reaching PyPI. Worse, it would break the point of the rehearsal: the file uploaded to TestPyPI would no longer be the file PyPI receives. The TestPyPI `ueler` project simply stays registered and stops receiving uploads — TestPyPI does not reclaim names for inactivity, and PyPI's decision about `ueler` has nothing to do with activity on TestPyPI.
- **`tools/check_release_tag.py` needed no change, which was checked rather than assumed.** Its wheel parser splits on `-` and takes index 1, and its sdist parser takes `rsplit("-", 1)`; the renamed artifacts normalise to `ueler_viewer-0.5.0a2-py3-none-any.whl` and `ueler_viewer-0.5.0a2.tar.gz`, so both still yield `0.5.0a2`. Confirmed by running it against the real build: all four declarations agree.
- **A pending Trusted Publisher does not reserve a project name.** The `ueler-viewer` pending publisher has existed on PyPI since 2026-08-10, yet `/simple/ueler-viewer/` still returns 404 — so no project object exists and the name is claimable by anyone. By the same argument the #79 audit used to rule out "registered with no releases" (an empty project would still have a simple-index page), the name is created and claimed only by the **first upload**. Consequence for the release: the first tagged upload *is* the reservation, on both indexes.
- **Two distributions can own the same import package, and one of them is on this machine.** `ueler` and `ueler-viewer` both install `ueler/`, and pip does not know they are the same project, so installing one over the other leaves two `dist-info` directories claiming the same files and uninstalling either can delete files the other still lists. Not hypothetical: the development env still holds an editable `ueler 0.3.0a0` (`site-packages/ueler-0.3.0a0.dist-info`, plus its `__editable__` finder and `.pth`), so a `pip install -e .` after this change produces exactly that collision — `pip uninstall ueler` first. The README and `docs/installation.md` upgrade paths now say so.
- **Documentation realigned with the index that actually serves UELer.** `docs/installation.md`'s "Option A — Install from PyPI" documented `pip install ueler` as though it worked; it is now "Install from TestPyPI" with the `ueler-viewer` commands, an admonition stating the install-name/import-name split up front, and the extras spelled as `"ueler-viewer[ark]"` / `"ueler-viewer[docs]"`. `README.md` leads with the same split, and `docs/develop-notes/packaging.md` records the decision, the one-name-on-both-indexes rationale and the pending-publisher finding.
- **Also fixed the install failure a user actually hit: `No matching distribution found for scikit-image>=0.19`.** Nothing to do with the pin — TestPyPI hosts an **empty** `scikit-image` project (`/simple/scikit-image/` returns 200 with zero links), so any resolve that consults TestPyPI alone finds no candidates, and `scikit-image` is the first entry in `dependencies` and therefore the first to fail. Two triggers, both now documented with fixes: `--extra-index-url https://pypi.org/simple/` dropped from the command — easy when the README's multi-line block with `\` continuations is pasted, so both install commands are now **one line** — and `uv pip install`, whose default `--index-strategy first-index` stops at the empty TestPyPI page and never falls back to PyPI, needing `--index-strategy unsafe-best-match`.
- **Planning and analysis:** `dev_note/issue_tracking/issue79_dist_name_rename.md`, linked from blocker **C4** of `issue79_pypi_release_readiness.md`, which now records the decision and the `euler` finding. It also carries the exit ramp should PyPI ever grant `ueler`: flip the one line back and publish a final `ueler-viewer` shim depending on `ueler` (the `sklearn` → `scikit-learn` pattern), without yanking the interim releases.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **1073 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped". Plus `python -m build` (both artifacts named `ueler_viewer-*`), `twine check --strict` (both PASSED), `check_release_tag.py --no-tag --require-dist` against the real build, and the wheel installed into a fresh venv and imported from outside the repository — `import ueler`, `import ueler.viewer.main_viewer` and the packaged `ready.png` asset all fine.

**A "Display Settings" page, because the defaults are MIBI defaults**

- **Every viewing control was already documented; the setup path was not.** Channel selection, contrast sliders, marker sets, mask and annotation overlays, cache size and pixel size are each described somewhere across `basic-usage.md`, `user-interface.md` and the FAQ, but only as reference fragments — nothing told a new user *which* of them to set before trusting what they see, and the launch-environment half of the answer existed only as FAQ troubleshooting, i.e. written for someone whose viewer is already broken. `docs/tutorials/display-settings.md` is that missing ordered walkthrough, placed in *Essentials* between **User Interface** and **Regions of Interest**.
- **The single highest-value thing it says: the pixel size is wrong for you unless you run MIBI.** `pixel_size_inttext` defaults to `390` (`ueler/viewer/ui_components.py:707`) — the MIBI pixel pitch, applied silently to every dataset. It drives the scale bar in the viewer *and* in every batch-exported image, so a non-MIBI user exports figures carrying a wrong scale bar with no warning anywhere in the UI. The page names 390 nm (MIBI) and 1000 nm (IMC) as examples, says to read the value off the acquisition metadata otherwise, and recommends **0** — which omits the scale bar entirely — over a guess, on the grounds that no scale bar is more honest than a wrong one.
- **Contrast is explained by where the default comes from, not by "move the sliders".** The initial display range is 0 to the channel's **99.9th percentile** (`da.nanpercentile(..., 99.9)`, `ueler/data_loader.py:132`), so the brightest 0.1 % of pixels saturate by design. That single fact turns the FAQ's all-black/all-white answer from trial and error into a decision: all-black means a bright artefact dragged the percentile up, so lower **Max**; all-white means real signal fills the frame, so raise **Min**. Two consequences that surprise people are stated explicitly — the **Max** slider's upper bound *is* the percentile-derived maximum, so the top 0.1 % cannot be dialled back in; and that bound is a running maximum, so it grows when a brighter FOV or map tile is first rendered (`_map_lazy_stats_enabled`, `merge_channel_max`).
- **Two claims from the planning document were wrong in the code and did not reach the page.** The plan recorded that chip order sets compositing precedence ("the last chip paints on top") and that **Downsample** only affects OME-TIFF sources. Neither holds: `_composite_channels` accumulates `composite += normalised * colour` and clips once (`ueler/rendering/engine.py:420`), so blending is **additive and order-independent** — the chip order controls the control-row order, the legend and the grid panes, nothing visual — and the downsample factor is chosen for every source from the drawn viewport (`calculate_downsample_factor` against `DOWNSAMPLE_MAX_DIMENSION = 2048`, `ueler/viewer/image_display.py:288`), with only the *pyramid-level push* being OME-TIFF-specific (`main_viewer.py:1984`). The page documents the real rule, including that the toggle does nothing at all on images at or below 2048 px, such as a standard 1024² FOV.
- **Cache guidance is arithmetic the reader can apply, not a house number.** `FOV pixels × channels opened × bytes per pixel × cached FOVs`, worked through for a 1024² uint16 FOV (~12 MB, so the default 100 is ~1.2 GB) and for 4000² tiles (~190 MB each, where the default will exhaust most machines). Paired with a note that unchecking a channel's visibility skips its compositing but does not release it — the data stays with the cached FOV until eviction.
- **`widget_states.json` is documented as a thing you can reset.** The FAQ lists the file; the page says *when* it is written (on every widget change once initialised, `main_viewer.py:5392`), what it restores on the next launch of the same `base_folder`, that plugins keep their own `<Plugin Name>_widget_states.json`, and that deleting it is how you escape a session that ended in a strange state — without touching ROIs, palettes, marker sets or export configs.
- **Cross-links rather than duplication:** `mkdocs.yml` nav, a row in the `tutorials/index.md` Essentials table and its "New here?" tip, a pointer from `basic-usage.md` §5 into the contrast section, a next-step from `getting-started.md`, and two FAQ answers that now explain the percentile instead of restating the fix. Verified with `mkdocs build --strict` (which fails on broken internal links) plus a manual check that every deep anchor used resolves in the built HTML.
- **Deferred:** screenshots. The site has exactly one image today, and cropped shots of the Channels accordion and Advanced Settings would help this page more than any other — worth adding once the accordion layout settles. See `dev_note/issue_tracking/doc_display_settings_page.md`.

**The home page carries the wide UELer logo**

- `docs/img/UELer_icon_wide.png` (the owl plus the expanded "Unified Exploratory Linked Viewer" wordmark) is now the banner at the top of `docs/index.md`. The artwork is black text on transparency, which would be all but invisible against Material's slate scheme, so it is rendered on an explicit white rounded panel — invisible against the light scheme's white page, and a legible card in dark mode.

**The interactive scatter is now the default everywhere, VS Code included (#122)**

- **The default was environment-dependent, and only one environment lost out.** `ChartDisplay.__init__` read `UELER_SCATTER_BACKEND`, but when the variable was unset it chose `"static" if os.environ.get("VSCODE_PID") else "widget"`. So JupyterLab and the classic Notebook got the interactive `jupyter-scatter` renderer while VS Code silently got a static Matplotlib figure with no linked brushing — recoverable only by knowing to set `os.environ["UELER_SCATTER_BACKEND"] = "widget"` *before* launching. That is the confusion the issue reported: an opt-in that users had no way to discover from the UI.
- **Why the special case existed, and why it can go.** The `VSCODE_PID` branch came from #64, where `jupyter-scatter` widgets intermittently failed to render in the VS Code webview and a blank output was worse than a static plot. The widgets render there now, so the trade is inverted: the fallback costs every VS Code user their interactivity to protect against a failure that no longer happens. `default_backend` is therefore the literal `"widget"`, and nothing in the resolution path inspects the environment beyond the variable itself.
- **`UELER_SCATTER_BACKEND` is kept, and flips from an opt-in to an opt-out.** `UELER_SCATTER_BACKEND=static` still selects the Matplotlib scatter, which is worth keeping for headless renders and for anyone who hits a webview that genuinely cannot host the widget. The two existing guards are untouched: the value is lower-cased before comparison, and anything outside `{"widget", "static"}` falls back to `widget` rather than erroring.
- **The fallback notice described a state that is now unreachable by accident.** It read "Interactive scatter widgets are disabled in this environment" and pointed at `UELER_SCATTER_BACKEND=widget` — advice that only ever fires when the user asked for `static` in the first place. It now reads "Static scatter backend active" and says to unset the variable and restart the kernel.
- **5 new tests in `tests/test_chart_footer_behavior.py`** (`ScatterBackendDefaultTests`), checked for being non-vacuous — restoring the old `VSCODE_PID` expression fails `test_vscode_no_longer_forces_the_static_fallback` and nothing else. They cover the bare default, `VSCODE_PID` no longer forcing `static`, `static` still honoured with and without `VSCODE_PID`, an unrecognised value falling back to `widget`, and case-insensitivity.
- **Four doc pages stated the old behaviour and would now actively mislead**, so `docs/faq.md`, `docs/tutorials/scatter-histogram.md`, `docs/installation.md` and `docs/develop-notes/viewer-runtime.md` were corrected — the FAQ entry in particular changes from "The scatter plot is not shown in VS Code" to "Do I need to set `UELER_SCATTER_BACKEND`?", whose answer is now simply no.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **1073 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped".

**Follow-up: a finished shape no longer disappears until it is saved**

- **Reported as "I clicked `Finish` and the polyline vanished; it was saved, but only became visible again after loading the ROI back".** Exactly right, and the cause was a gap between two layers that each behaved correctly on their own: `Finish` calls `disable_polyline_editor`, which removes the editor's own artists, while `refresh_shape_overlay` drew only the shapes read back from the ROI **table**. Between finishing and saving, a shape belonged to neither — the editor had let go of it and the table had never heard of it — so nothing on the canvas was drawing it, even though `_shape_points` still held it and `Save shape` still worked.
- **Fix: the overlay now draws the working copy as well as the saved shapes.** `refresh_shape_overlay` appends `_shape_points` whenever it is non-empty and no drawing session is active; during a session the editor's artists already show it, and appending would draw the shape twice.
- **The same gap, mirrored, when editing an existing shape.** With the working copy now drawn, a shape loaded through `Edit` would appear twice — once live from the editor and once stale from the table. `_visible_shape_geometries` therefore skips the record named by `_shape_edit_roi_id` while a working copy exists, and `_start_shape_edit` refreshes the overlay so the stale copy disappears the moment editing begins.
- **After a successful save the working copy is released** (`_shape_points` is cleared), so the shape has exactly one source of truth — the table — instead of being drawn from both at identical coordinates. The summary line is rewritten as `Saved …` rather than falling back to the idle text.
- **`Save shape` now finishes the drawing for you.** Requiring `Finish` first was the trap that produced the report: forgetting it left the shape apparently gone and the button apparently dead. Saving mid-draw implies finishing, so `_on_shape_save` ends the session itself before storing.
- **8 new tests in `tests/test_line_roi.py`** (`FinishedShapeStaysVisibleTests`), checked for being non-vacuous — disabling the working-copy branch fails exactly the two that describe it. They cover a finished-unsaved shape still being drawn, the working copy *not* being drawn during a session, cancelling removing it, an edited shape hiding its stale saved copy, saving releasing the working copy so the shape is drawn once, saving straight from a live drawing session, the summary after saving, and `Show shapes on canvas` staying off throughout.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **1068 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped".

**Line and polygon ROIs are back, this time inside the ROI manager**

- **What was lost, and how.** UELer once had a `Region annotation` plugin (`viewer/plugin/region_annotation.py`, whose class was confusingly named `RunFlowsom` — a copy-paste leftover) that let the user click a chain of points onto the canvas, drag them, lasso-select them, nudge them with the arrow keys and auto-save them to `<folder>/<fov>.csv`. It arrived in `7df488f` (2025-10-15) with the transfer from `UELer_alpha` and vanished in `9985621` (2026-04-09, first shipped in v0.4.0), the `viewer/` → `ueler/viewer/` migration: every other plugin was copied into `ueler/viewer/plugin/`, this one was not, and the old tree was then deleted. Because `dynamically_load_plugins()` discovers plugins by listing the plugin directory, a file missing from the new one produces no import error — the tab simply stopped existing, and nothing replaced it.
- **Rebuilt as a row kind in the ROI table, not as a plugin again.** The old plugin owned a private CSV that nothing else read, which is exactly why losing it was invisible and why it never gained tags, thumbnails, filtering or export. `ROI_COLUMNS` therefore gains **`roi_kind`** (`""`/`"view"` for the viewport bookmarks it has always stored, `"line"` for a drawn shape) and **`geometry`** (`{"type", "closed", "points"}` as JSON), and the tag editor, browser gallery, expression filter, CSV import/export and batch export all apply to shapes with no new machinery. JSON in one cell is the convention this table already uses for `mask_visibility` and `mask_painter_state`. An empty `roi_kind` means "view", so every CSV written before this change loads unmigrated; both columns are registered in `_ensure_dataframe`'s string set, which otherwise back-fills a missing column with `0.0` and would have turned `geometry` into a number.
- **Open polylines and closed polygons share one `roi_kind`** and are told apart by `geometry["closed"]`. One kind keeps filtering to a single predicate — `is_shape_record()`, used by the export filter and the canvas overlay — while `shape_display_kind()` still names them separately in the label and the UI. A record carrying geometry but no `roi_kind` is read as a shape, so a hand-edited CSV behaves sensibly.
- **The bounding box is what makes every existing consumer work unchanged.** A shape still writes `x`/`y`/`width`/`height` and `x_min`…`y_max`, derived from its vertices, so `center_on_roi`, `render_roi_to_array`, `_render_roi_tile`, `_render_map_roi_tile` and the batch-export job builder treat it exactly like a viewport bookmark and needed no changes at all. **Exporting a line ROI therefore exports its bounding-box region**, which is the requested behaviour and came for free. A straight horizontal or vertical line has a zero-extent box and `_render_map_roi_tile` rejects `x_max <= x_min`, so `geometry_bounds()` pads every box to at least 8 px per axis plus 4 px of margin, clamped into the canvas and never negative.
- **Drawing uses Matplotlib artists, deliberately, and not the pixel-baking path.** `update_patches` paints mask highlights *into* the RGB array and any later `set_data` erases them (the #119 ordering bug). Artists are a separate layer, no code path in the viewer clears these axes, and a shape drawn as a `Line2D` therefore survives zoom, pan, FOV switches and every mask repaint. This is the one thing the old plugin got right. `enable_polyline_editor` / `disable_polyline_editor` follow the one-shot lifecycle of the existing `enable_roi_selector` (a `RectangleSelector` that has had **no callers** since it was written) and `enable_lasso_selector`, including the toolbar release the lasso needed — an active zoom or pan holds `canvas.widgetlock` and silently swallows every event. That release is now one shared `_release_navigation_tool()` instead of two copies.
- **Interactions:** left-click appends a vertex, left-drag on a vertex moves it, right-click deletes the nearest one, `delete` drops the last, `ctrl+z`/`ctrl+y` undo and redo, `enter` finishes and `escape` cancels. A drag that starts on empty canvas is treated as a pan and adds nothing; a click that lands on a vertex without moving it pops the undo entry pushed on press, so the history holds no no-op steps. `on_mouse_click` gained a `_polyline_active` guard beside the existing `_lasso_active` one, so placing vertices does not also select the cells underneath.
- **The UI is a *Line / polygon ROI* block inside the existing ROI editor tab** — no new tab, no new plugin: `Draw` / `Edit` / `Finish` / `Cancel`, `Undo` / `Redo` / `Save shape`, a `Closed shape (polygon)` toggle, a `Show shapes on canvas` toggle, and a live readout of vertex count with length (or perimeter) in px and µm. `Edit` centres on the selected shape before loading it, since vertices live in the coordinate space of their own FOV or map. Saving is explicit, unlike the old plugin's save-on-every-keystroke, matching how every other ROI here is created. An in-progress shape is cancelled on a FOV change or a map-mode transition, because the coordinate space moves out from under it.
- **`Update` no longer means the same thing for both kinds.** For a viewport ROI it still overwrites the stored bounds with the current viewport; for a shape it updates only the metadata, since overwriting the box would detach it from the vertices. Geometry changes go through `Edit` → `Save shape`. The location and preset fields the three save paths share moved into one `_common_metadata_fields()`, so a shape stores the same marker set and palettes as a bookmark and its thumbnail renders identically.
- **Shapes are visible where ROIs are already browsed and exported.** `_draw_shape_on_tile` stamps the path onto the rendered thumbnail (a shape tile would otherwise be indistinguishable from a bookmark of the same box), and the batch-export ROI list gained an **`Include line/polygon ROIs`** checkbox, on by default, beside `Current FOV only`.
- **Out of scope by decision, not oversight:** per-cell distance to the shape — the likely original scientific purpose — is deferred, confirmed with the developer. Spline fitting is not coming back either; the old `fit_spline()` was an empty `pass`, so nothing is lost. **Known limitation:** `_ensure_dataframe` ends with `df[ROI_COLUMNS]`, so an *older* UELer reading a CSV written by this version silently drops `roi_kind` and `geometry` — forward-compatible, not backward.
- **74 new tests in `tests/test_line_roi.py`**, covering the geometry helpers (round trip, malformed and `NaN` payloads, points dropped individually, open vs closed length, the padded box for horizontal, vertical and single-point shapes, clamping), persistence (a shape reloaded from disk, a legacy CSV without the two columns, export/import, a shape and a bookmark coexisting), the editor driven through its real event handlers against a real Agg axes (placement, drag, right-click delete, undo/redo including the moved-vertex and no-op cases, the pan-gesture guard, `enter`/`escape`, prefilled editing, the closing segment), the artist overlay (including that it survives an `img.set_data`, which is the whole reason it is an artist), the click guard, the thumbnail stamp, the export filter, and the plugin's validation, summary text and view-scoped shape lookup. Two existing test doubles were extended rather than worked around — `DummyROIManager` gained the `get_roi` that `_update_selected_roi` now needs, and the batch-export UI stub gained the new checkbox — and `ROIManagerMapModeTests._make_plugin` now delegates to the module-level `make_plugin(viewer)` instead of duplicating thirty lines of plugin state that had to be updated in both places.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **1060 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped" (986 before). **Not covered by tests, to confirm in a notebook:** that the pointer gestures actually place and drag vertices in a live ipympl canvas, and that the shape overlay repaints as expected after a zoom — the dev environment has no browser, so the tests drive the event handlers directly rather than the frontend. See `dev_note/issue_tracking/line_roi_support.md`.

**A handful of cells selected in the image is now visible in the histogram (issue #135 reply)**
- **Follow main viewer** put the image's own selection into the histogram, but what it drew was easy to miss. The selection is rendered as its own distribution — a second bar series on the *same* y-axis as the "All" bars — which is the right encoding for what it was built for (a gate covering a good fraction of the cells, #112/#127) and the wrong one for what #135 introduced. Clicking five cells out of 80 000 draws a bar 0.006 % of the plot height: correct, and invisible. The user sees nothing change and reads the feature as broken.
- **Below a visibility threshold the histogram switches encoding rather than exaggerating the bar.** Every bin holding at least one selected cell is now tinted over its **whole** height, which answers "where are my cells" — the only question a five-cell selection can answer, since the shape of a five-cell distribution is noise. The proportional overlay is still drawn on top of the tint, so the true count stays readable and no bar height stops meaning what the y-axis says. The legend names the third series **Bins with selection**, and `legend.click_policy = "hide"` already lets it be switched off per figure.
- **The threshold is 5 % of the tallest bar on that channel, not 5 % of the cells.** The reply proposed "when selected cells are less than 5% of the total"; the rule keeps the 5 % but measures it where visibility is actually decided — the ratio of peak bar heights *is* the fraction of the figure height the selection gets. Counting cells instead misfires in both directions: 3 % of the cells concentrated in one bin is perfectly visible and would be tinted for nothing, while 8 % of them spread over 200 bins is invisible and would not be. The decision is taken per figure for the same reason: one selection is routinely sharp on the marker that defines it and diffuse on every other.
- **Rejected: scaling the overlay onto a secondary y-axis.** It preserves the shape of the selected distribution, which the tint does not, but the two ranges desynchronise the moment the user wheel-zooms in y — the main range is a `DataRange1d` the kernel does not track — and it quietly changes what a bar height means. The tint has neither problem and is what the reply asked for.
- **It is a data write, not a rebuild.** The tint is a third `ColumnDataSource` per figure, written by `_refresh_overlays` alongside the existing one, so it costs one more `.data` assignment per channel and cannot disturb a zoom, a pan or another channel's gate marker — the invariant #127 established. The per-channel full counts are cached in the source dict at build time so a selection never re-bins the column. The new renderer joins the `selection_glyph`/`nonselection_glyph` pinning loop, so a box-select gesture still cannot mute it.
- **`Mark faint selections` in the *Histogram* tab**, on by default, switches the behaviour off for anyone who wants the strictly proportional reading. Toggling it re-runs `_refresh_overlays()` and never replots; it persists with every other widget state.
- **17 new tests in `tests/test_issue135_faint_selection_highlight.py`**, verified non-vacuous: disabling the threshold branch fails 8 of them. The rule is tested as pure arithmetic (`_faint_selection_tops`: the occupied bins and only those, tinted to the full bar height; nothing at or above the threshold; nothing for an empty selection; empty data safe; and the cells-vs-heights distinction pinned directly) and then through real Bokeh sources (one clicked cell tints one bin to 360 while the honest overlay still reports 1; 500 cells are left alone; one selection tinted on `intensity` and not on `area`; the checkbox toggles the tint without replotting and without touching the selection). The fixture is deliberately a *peaked* distribution — a uniform one cannot reproduce the bug at all, since one cell in a 20-cell bar is already 5 %.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **986 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped".

**Cells selected in the main viewer can now be mapped into the plots (issue #135)**
- Selections only ever flowed one way. A scatter lasso, a histogram gate or a heatmap cluster could be pushed *into* the image through each plugin's **Main viewer** checkbox (#119/#129), but the cells a user picked **in** the image never reached the plots — except through the one-shot **Trace** / **Trace cluster** buttons, which have to be pressed again after every selection and only look at the active FOV (Trace) or the first selected cell (Trace cluster).
- **No new event was needed.** `ImageDisplay` already broadcasts `inform_plugins("on_selection_change")` after a click, a ctrl-click, a lasso and a clear; only the cell-table editor listened (to enable its Apply button). The feature is three new receivers on that existing broadcast, each gated by its own checkbox.
- **The checkbox is named `Follow main viewer`**, in the *Linked plugins* tab of the Scatter plot, Histogram and Heatmap plugins. The issue proposed "Receive Selection" and asked for a better name: the other checkboxes in that tab are named after the *target* of a push (**Main viewer**, **Cell gallery**, **Scatter plot**, **Histogram**), so the name that disambiguates is the one that reads as a direction — and "follow" also distinguishes it from the one-shot **Trace** button sitting a tab away. Off by default, and remembered per plugin like every other control.
- **One shared translator, `_chart_common.viewer_selection_indices`,** is the inverse of `sync_mask_highlights_from_selection`: it turns the `(fov, mask, mask_id)` triples in `image_display.selected_masks_label` back into the FOV-independent cell-table row indices every plot speaks. It matches on `(fov, label)` — the pair the cell-table editor already uses, since the triple records the mask the *pixel* was hit in while the cell table keys on the label id — and matches per FOV, so a map-mode selection spanning several FOVs resolves correctly instead of collapsing onto the active one. It returns an empty set rather than raising on anything missing, because `inform_plugins` swallows `AttributeError` and a hook that threw one would fail invisibly.
- **The receive path never pushes back, and that is the one design point that matters.** Each plugin's selection entry point ends by writing mask highlights into the viewer when its "Main viewer" box is ticked. Reached from `on_selection_change` that would call `set_mask_ids`, which *replaces* `selected_masks_label` with the current-FOV projection of what it had just received — silently dropping the other FOVs' cells of a map-mode lasso and re-deriving, badly, the selection the user had made by hand. `_commit_scatter_selection` and `show_external_selection` therefore take a `push_highlight` keyword (default `True`, so every existing caller including the heatmap→histogram link is unchanged) and the receive path passes `False`. The heatmap hook likewise skips the `highlight_cells()` call that ends `trace_cluster`.
- **The heatmap reuses its scatter-link machinery rather than `trace_cluster`.** `_map_indices_to_cluster_positions` + `_apply_cluster_highlights` already turn a set of row indices into highlighted cluster bands — that is how the scatter→heatmap link works — so a multi-cell selection spanning several clusters highlights all of them, where `trace_cluster` handles a single cell.
- **Ticking the box applies the selection already on screen**, so the link takes effect without re-clicking a cell. Unticking deliberately leaves the plot as it is: unlike the outline the viewer draws (#129), a selection *in a plot* is normal, user-clearable plot state, and wiping it on untick would also wipe a selection made in that plot itself.
- **Deliberately not covered:** the Chart (heatmap) plugin, whose rows are clusters rather than cells, so a cell-row selection has no meaning in its index; and the cell gallery, which is not a plot and is already a push *target* of every plugin. `PluginBase` gained an `on_selection_change` no-op so the hook is discoverable next to `on_fov_change` and friends — behaviour-neutral, since `inform_plugins` already swallowed the `AttributeError`.
- **34 new tests in `tests/test_issue135_follow_main_viewer_selection.py`**, checked for being non-vacuous: removing the `push_highlight` guard makes the scatter and histogram "never written back to" tests fail immediately. Coverage is split between the translator (single FOV, map-mode multi-FOV, a label matched only within its own FOV, unknown ids dropped, the mask name ignored, string-typed label columns, non-default index, and the defensive paths returning an empty set) and one matrix per plugin (unlinked ignores, linked mirrors, the viewer is never written back to, clearing the image clears the plot, ticking applies, unticking does not wipe), plus the hook wiring itself.
- Verified: `python tools/run_test_suite.py --max-skips 0` → **969 tests, OK**, "No tests were skipped". **Not covered by tests, to confirm in a notebook:** that the points actually redraw as selected in a live jscatter widget and that the Bokeh overlay repaints — the tests assert the selection state each plugin publishes, not what the frontend paints.

### v0.5.0-alpha1
**README: installation points at TestPyPI, and the update summary is trimmed to this version**
- **Installation now installs from TestPyPI, not PyPI.** The distribution name `ueler` is still rejected by PyPI's prohibited-names list (**C4** in [`dev_note/issue_tracking/issue79_pypi_release_readiness.md`](../dev_note/issue_tracking/issue79_pypi_release_readiness.md)), so `pip install ueler` does not work yet and the README said otherwise. Option A and *Upgrade UELer* now use `--index-url https://test.pypi.org/simple/` with `--extra-index-url https://pypi.org/simple/` — the extra index is not optional, since TestPyPI does not mirror the runtime dependencies — plus `--pre` for the pre-release version. The section states why, and that it reverts to a plain `pip install ueler` once the name is settled.
- **Removed "Updating Your Environment for v0.1.7-alpha (or earlier) users" entirely.** It told users to `micromamba install dask` / `dask-image` by hand; both have been declared dependencies for many versions, and v0.1.7-alpha predates the current package layout. *Upgrade UELer* survives as a subsection of *Installation*, which is where it belongs now that the migration text above it is gone.
- **"New Update" now covers only v0.5.0-alpha1.** It had accumulated the whole v0.5.0-alpha entry (BSD relicensing, CI, PyPI preparation, the dtype fix, the test-bootstrap opt-in, the legacy-alias removal) alongside this version's two fixes, so a reader could not tell what the release they just installed actually changed. Trimmed to the grid-view cell-locating fix (#134) and the scatter-matrix axis convention (#133), matching this section of the log; the pointer beneath it now reads "v0.5.0-alpha and before" instead of "v0.4.4 and before".
- Documentation only — no code touched, so no test run is implicated. Note that the docs site (`docs/`) still carries the old `pip install ueler` instructions and was left alone here.

**Locating a single cell now works in the channel grid view (issue #134)**
- With the channel grid view active, asking the viewer to locate a cell — Go-To, a cell-gallery tile, a scatter or heatmap point — did nothing at all: no error, no movement. Centring on a saved ROI from the ROI manager had the same defect.
- **The coordinates were never wrong.** The issue's working hypothesis was a coordinate-transform problem introduced by the split panes; it is not. All panes are `sharex`/`sharey` and are positioned by `set_extent` in full-resolution pixel coordinates, exactly like the single-pane display. What goes wrong is *which axes* the target window is written to.
- **`focus_on_cell()` and `center_on_roi()` write only into `image_display.ax`, which is the hidden figure while grid mode is active.** Grid mode does not reuse that figure — `on_grid_view_toggle` builds a `GridChannelDisplay` with its own canvas and its own axes, hides `image_output` and shows `grid_output`. The only coupling between the two was one-directional: `GridChannelDisplay._on_draw()` copies the *grid's* limits back into `image_display.ax` after each pan/zoom, so `get_axis_limits_with_padding()` keeps working. Nothing carried limits in the opposite direction, so programmatic navigation moved an axes nobody could see, no draw event fired on the grid canvas, and the panes never re-rendered. That is the exact shape of the report — silent, not broken.
- **Fixed on the display side rather than in the navigation methods.** New `GridChannelDisplay.set_viewport(xlim, ylim)` — the public counterpart to the existing `get_viewport()` — applies the limits to the primary axes (they reach every pane through `sharex`/`sharey`), mirrors them into `image_display.ax` so the padding helper agrees, recomputes the downsample factor for the new zoom level, and re-renders the panes. A new `ImageMaskViewer._sync_grid_viewport()` bridges the two, called from `center_on_roi()` and from both exit paths of `focus_on_cell()` (the stitched-map branch and the per-FOV branch) right after the limits are set; it is a no-op when grid mode is off. The navigation methods stay the single source of truth for *where* to look — including the map-mode coordinate resolution, which is why locating a cell works in grid mode on a stitched map too — and the grid simply follows.
- **Recomputing the downsample factor is part of the fix, not a refinement.** Locating a cell is a large zoom step; without it the panes would re-render the new region at the factor chosen for the previous, much wider view. The computation was lifted out of `_on_draw()` into a shared `_sync_downsample_factor()` so both the interactive and the programmatic path honour `DOWNSAMPLE_MAX_DIMENSION` and the "disable downsampling" checkbox identically.
- **Re-entry was the one trap worth naming.** `set_viewport` ends in a `draw_idle()`, whose `draw_event` calls `_on_draw`, which re-renders. It records the new centre in `_prev_cx`/`_prev_cy` *before* scheduling the draw, so `_on_draw`'s existing unchanged-viewport check recognises the event as already handled. `test_subsequent_draw_event_does_not_reenter` pins that.
- **Twelve new tests in `tests/test_channel_grid_view.py`**, and the fix was checked for being non-vacuous the direct way: with `_sync_grid_viewport` stubbed back to a no-op, `focus_on_cell` leaves the grid at `((0, 100), (100, 0))` while `image_display.ax` moves to `(40, 60), (50, 30)` — the pre-fix symptom reproduced exactly. Coverage is split between `set_viewport` itself (limits applied and propagated to every pane, `image_display.ax` mirrored, panes re-rendered, factor recomputed, no re-entry, safe before `initialized`) and the viewer bridge (grid follows `focus_on_cell` and `center_on_roi`, unchanged behaviour and no sync attempted with grid mode off).
- Verified: `python -m unittest discover -s tests` → **935 tests, OK**.

**Scatter matrix: rows now share a y-axis and columns share an x-axis (issue #133)**
- "Plot all pairs" in the Scatter plugin lays the pairwise plots out as an upper-triangular matrix over the selected channels (#113/#118). The axis assignment inside that matrix was the transpose of the standard scatter-matrix convention: the plot at row `i`, column `j` carried `channels[i]` on **x** and `channels[j]` on **y**, so reading across a row held x fixed and reading down a column held y fixed — the opposite of every SPLOM the convention comes from, and the reason the grid read as unintuitive.
- Fixed by swapping which channel of each `itertools.combinations` pair becomes x and which becomes y: the plot at `(i, j)` now shows `channels[i]` on **y** and `channels[j]` on **x**. A row therefore shares its y-axis and a column shares its x-axis. The geometry of the matrix is untouched — same upper triangle, same `N-1` `1fr` CSS-grid columns, same leading blanks — only the axis roles flip.
- `_scatter_pairs` stores views keyed by `(x, y)`, so `_triangular_grid` had to be updated in step: the pairs it expects and the lookup for cell `(i, j)` are now `(channels[j], channels[i])`. Missing that would have silently broken the "is this matrix still complete?" check and dropped the layout to the `compose` fallback. The static Matplotlib fallback (used when jscatter is unavailable) plots the first pair with the same orientation, so the two backends agree.
- Regression test `test_matrix_rows_share_y_axis_and_columns_share_x_axis` pins the convention by mapping each grid cell back to its view's `(x, y)` pair — it asserts row 0 is `[(b,a), (c,a)]`, row 1 is `[blank, (c,b)]`, and that column 1 shares x. Verified non-vacuous: restoring the old orientation fails it, and takes two existing matrix-layout tests down with it.

### v0.5.0-alpha
**The 3.12 CI leg's first reading: a pandas-3 forward-compatibility bug, not a Python 3.12 incompatibility (issue #79)**
- Gate C added a `continue-on-error` Python 3.12 matrix leg specifically so the `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.13"` question would be settled by evidence. It ran on the first push and reported **19 errors** while `unit (py3.10)`, `unit (py3.11)` and `build + verify the distribution` all passed and the run as a whole stayed green — the leg behaved exactly as designed, which is the only reason this was a reading rather than a broken build.
- **All 19 errors were one error.** Every traceback ended in `TypeError: Cannot interpret '<StringDtype(na_value=nan)>' as a data type`, and every one passed through a single four-line closure: `_convert` inside `MaskPainterDisplay.apply_colors_to_masks`, which converts the class labels the widgets hand back (always strings) to the dtype of the identifier column so that `cell_table[identifier] == value` matches rows at all. It asked `np.issubdtype`.
- **It is not a 3.12 problem, and that was established by reproducing it on 3.10.** Setting `pd.options.future.infer_string = True` on this project's own environment — Python 3.10.19, pandas 2.3.3 — produces **19 identical errors**, an exact match with CI. What differs across the matrix is not the language but the resolver: **pandas 3.0 requires Python ≥ 3.11**, so the 3.12 leg was handed a pandas that had already made `StringDtype` the default for object string columns, and `np.issubdtype` raises `TypeError` on *every* pandas extension dtype.
- **So the tempting fix was the wrong one.** The open decision was framed as "add the 3.12 classifier, or tighten the bound to `<3.12`" — and tightening it would have made the symptom disappear without touching the cause. pandas 3 is installable on 3.11 too, so the same 19 errors were one dependency resolution away from a *supported* Python; the 3.11 leg passing is the resolver's current preference, not evidence of correctness.
- **The bug was already live on current pandas, which is the part that matters for users.** `np.issubdtype` also raises `TypeError` on `Int64Dtype`, `Float64Dtype` and `CategoricalDtype` — and `flatten_anndata` produces all three, since `obs` columns arrive categorical routinely. Painting a cell table whose identifier column is a `category` crashed on today's pandas, on every supported Python; no test covered it, so nothing said so.
- **Fix: `is_integer_column_dtype` / `is_float_column_dtype` in `ueler/cell_table.py`**, used by `mask_painter.py`. They ask `pandas.api.types` first, which understands numpy and extension dtypes alike, and fall back to `np.issubdtype` only for the minimal pandas stand-in in `tests/bootstrap.py`, whose `api.types` namespace carries a different, *value*-based set of helpers. Unknown objects return `False` rather than raising.
- **Categoricals are deliberately unwrapped to `.categories.dtype` rather than classified as "neither".** `pd.Series(pd.Categorical([1, 2])) == "1"` matches **0** rows where `== 1` matches 1, so treating a `category` column of integers as non-numeric would have traded a loud `TypeError` for a silently empty selection — a worse failure, because it looks like a result.
